##### Original admission operator-context bridge

This bounded continuation belongs to original flat piece4, not a new milestone
or public contract family. `RealmAdmissionOperatorContextBridge.js` connects
M1C's real context-consumer methods to already-issued original B4A and lifecycle
observations. It does not implement admission storage, policies, graph loading,
eligibility, the activation owner or a live provider.

The incompatibility was concrete: M1C calls `snapshot({ signal })` and
`assertCurrent({ operatorIdentity, operatorGeneration, signal })` with mutable
operation records. B4A's frozen public wire instead binds its first snapshot to
the original construction signal and subsequent currentness to the distinct
original work signal. Passing the M1C operation root directly or copying a
snapshot cannot establish that provenance. Both existing implementations and
their contracts remain byte-exact.

`createRealmAdmissionOperatorContextBridge()` accepts one frozen exact own-data
record with three fields: original `operatorContextAdapter`, original
`lifecycleComposition`, and original frozen five-field `currentnessRequest`.
The request binds `os.the-virtual-realm`, operator identity/generation, actual
issued lifecycle generation and the original native work root. Original private
companion capture authenticates both issuers and their shared composition before
capturing the already-published observation and already-issued allocation. No
snapshot reread, allocation, policy operation or new host owner occurs.

The frozen bridge contains only `operatorContextPort` and `close()`. Its private
service port has precisely `snapshot` and `assertCurrent`; it is not the public
B4A wire and does not enter the sixteen app dependencies. Each operation requires
exactly one plain own-data record, mutable or frozen, with respectively one or
three fields. Descriptor snapshots reject unknown, symbol, hidden, accessor and
inherited-enumerable fields without executing getters. Native operation signals
are cancellation-only and may differ from the retained original work root.
Actual native aborted-state checks ignore own `aborted/reason` getters, duck
signals, proxy signals and fabricated abort events. Valid calls return the exact
same original frozen six-field B4A snapshot, never a reconstructed context.

`captureRealmAdmissionOperatorContextSource(originalBridge)` is an original-only
private WeakMap lookup. Copies, proxies and matching public values do not receive
proof or operand reflection. Its frozen Source4 exposes `binding`, native
dependent `signal`, zero-argument `assertCurrent()` and three-argument
`assertBinding(originalAdapter, originalComposition, originalCurrentnessRequest)`.
Binding8 is scalar metadata: app, account/partition, operator/lifecycle generation,
local Realm, authority epoch and policy epoch. It exposes no root controller,
upstream close, storage, policy or eligibility capability. Another legitimate
bridge can be created over the same genuine cut; copied request values do not
satisfy an already-issued source's exact original-reference assertion.

Bridge lifetime joins its private terminal root, original observation, original
lifecycle and original work signals, never the completed construction root.
Actual upstream retirement or loss irreversibly stops the bridge. Terminal state
is committed and redundant retained references are cleared, then its own listener is detached,
before private abort callbacks or bounded diagnostics run. Synthetic events and
stopped event propagation cannot substitute for native dependent state. Currentness
rechecks both genuine original sources; diagnostics are fenced before returning
snapshot/currentness results. A canceled independent operation leaves a still-current
bridge intact. No refresh or revival path exists.

`close()` publishes one shared promise before synchronous callbacks and ends only
its own observation/listener state. Its frozen `{ closed: true }` receipt is not
upstream boot-owner cleanup, generation retirement or physical resource-release
evidence. It does not close the B4A adapter, lifecycle composition, StorageManager
or boot binding, and never aborts their shared roots. Diagnostics contain frozen
`status/count` only, with no account, snapshot, path, raw exception or authority.

The browser gate reuses the actual OPFS-backed boot/B4A fixture and actually
allocates/retires lifecycle generations. It invokes a real
`RealmPrivateBakeAdmissionService` constructor and its actual `_captureContext`
and `_assertContextCurrent` methods with matching genuine protected account
storage. Unused content, policy, trust, verifier and registry dependencies are
explicitly controlled rejecting ports, with zero calls required. The mismatched
account negative case is controlled. This is genuine context-consumer evidence,
not a full `admit`, `readHead` or `loadVerifiedPrivateBake` proof.

The follow-on map preserves an important distinction: existing M1C composition
storage checks establish shape/scope labels, not native protected-view provenance
for every supplied port. Factory-issued service identity would prove implementation
lineage, not automatically authenticate content, publication/selection ownership,
admission policy, trust, evidence, profile or verifier authority. The retained B4F

##### Original StorageManager service-view provenance

This continuation implements the next protected-storage prerequisite inside
original flat piece4. It adds `captureStorageOperatorServiceViewSource()` to
the existing StorageManager. It does not replace the storage view, create a new
admission service, change the sixteen app dependencies or activate a provider.
The private admission context bridge and public B4A/B4F contracts remain unchanged.
(Sources: `webgpu-os/storage/StorageManager.js`;
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmAdmissionOperatorContextBridge.js`.)

The distinction is necessary: `OperatorPrivateServiceStorageView.is()` proves
the lower-level factory issued a view, but that exported factory accepts supplied
scoped-storage/context callbacks. The new private issuance WeakMap enrolls only
the view returned by StorageManager's existing private binding seam. It retains
the original manager/private lifetime, scoped-storage binding, service scope,
registered descriptor, operator context/scope and bound signal. A borrowed binder
on a copied, forged or proxy manager has no original private lifetime and cannot
acquire the proof. The lower-level factory never enrolls its supplied storage.
(Sources: `webgpu-os/storage/StorageManager.js`;
`webgpu-os/kernel/OperatorPrivateServiceStorageView.js`.)

Capture accepts exactly one original view and performs private membership lookup
before operand reflection. Copies, inherited views, hostile/revoked proxies and
matching labels do not receive a proof. The first capture is lazy: ordinary
legacy binding adds no listeners and does not require the stronger proof. Capture
requires a non-null original context, a live native bound signal, exact current
scope identity and an actual native listener Set registration. Legacy null-context,
null-signal and duck-signal views remain compatible without this companion.
(Source: `webgpu-os/storage/StorageManager.js`.)

The same frozen Source4 is returned on repeated capture. It contains only
`binding`, native `signal`, zero-argument `assertCurrent()` and exactly-five-argument
`assertBinding(originalManager, originalOperatorContext, originalOperatorScope,
originalServiceDescriptor, originalBoundSignal)`. Binding3 contains scalar
`serviceId`, `accountId` and numeric `generation`. The binding assertion compares
original references; another genuine manager/context or copied scope with equal
labels cannot replace them. No source exposes storage operations, physical roots,
controllers, upstream close, content, policy or eligibility capabilities.
(Source: `webgpu-os/storage/StorageManager.js`.)

Currentness rechecks the original private manager lifetime and actual scoped-storage
binding. Captured context methods require the exact current scope, not equal labels.
Captured native abort getters ignore signal property shadows; proxy and duck
signals fail native branding. Fabricated abort events do not change native state.
A dependent native root joins the private terminal
controller with the original bound signal, so stopped propagation on the bound
root cannot conceal actual cancellation. This companion never dispatches through
replaceable context/view `assertCurrent` methods or performs storage I/O.
(Source: `webgpu-os/storage/StorageManager.js`.)

Lazy capture enrolls its pending state before registration callouts. It verifies
the original own-data listener Set identity and intrinsic membership after
registration, and rechecks them on both sides of diagnostics. A no-op listener
hook or registration that inserts then throws cannot publish a live proof.
An independent limit admits64 live captured sources per original manager.
Repeated capture consumes no additional slot. Cancellation retires the source
and releases its slot; this limit does not restrict legacy view binding.
(Source: `webgpu-os/storage/StorageManager.js`.)

Terminal preparation commits irreversible stopped state, clears retained issuance
references and removes live-set membership before disposer, abort or diagnostic
callbacks. Cleanup removes its native signal listener and original context
registration; captured native `Set.delete` provides fallback if the replaceable
disposer throws before removal. Manager close prepares every retained source
before dispatch, so a callback cannot treat a sibling as live. Its existing
owner-drain, return and error paths are preserved. Caller roots, context, boot
binding and other managers are never closed by this companion. There is no
refresh, revival or upstream-cleanup receipt.
(Source: `webgpu-os/storage/StorageManager.js`.)

Diagnostics are frozen `status/count` records with fixed `created`, `current`
and `stopped` statuses. Errors expose fixed frozen codes without raw causes.
Final native context, registration, lifetime and cancellation fences follow
diagnostics before results escape. The original public binding method,
StorageManager constructor and scoped-storage class have independent unchanged
section hashes. Removing only the two new prepare/dispatch statements restores
the original normalized close-method hash. No source imports or cycles were added.
(Sources: `webgpu-os/storage/StorageManager.js`;
`tests/virtual-realm/test_m2_storage_service_view_source.py`.)

The gate reuses actual OPFS-backed boot/B4A fixtures and a genuine coordinator.
A separate original manager issues the tested views. It covers all eight
registered service identities, exact-reference rejection, native cancellation,
scope retirement, reentrant manager closure, registration/disposer faults and
capacity recovery. An absent-only bounded control write has exact OPFS readback;
the persisted selection and local-view-policy heads remain unchanged. The
controlled lower-level backend is used only to prove it receives no stronger
source and zero operations. There is no fabricated admission/eligibility owner.
(Source: `tests/virtual-realm/m2-storage-service-view-source.test.js`.)

This proof establishes original StorageManager issuance and retained original
references, not authentic boot-context selection, arbitrary backend isolation,
publication/selection ownership or complete admission. OperatorContext's captured
prototype methods still read public instance fields; they are not a constructor
issuer registry. A caller can replace StorageManager's backend fields, and this
proof does not certify those replacements. The genuine OPFS test is execution
evidence for its actual fixture, not a universal backend-authority guarantee.
(Sources: `webgpu-os/kernel/OperatorContext.js`;
`webgpu-os/storage/StorageManager.js`.)

##### Original admission protected-storage cut binding

This add-only continuation stays inside original flat piece4. The private
[protected-storage binding](../../../webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmAdmissionProtectedStorageBinding.js)
joins the original StorageManager-issued admission view to the already observed
admission bridge and its actual issued lifecycle. It performs no storage I/O,
new observation, allocation, policy operation or provider installation.

`createRealmAdmissionProtectedStorageBinding()` accepts exactly one frozen,
own-data, flat ten-field record: `storageManager`, `storageView`,
`operatorContext`, `operatorScope`, `serviceDescriptor`, `boundSignal`,
`admissionContextBridge`, `operatorContextAdapter`, `lifecycleComposition`, and
`currentnessRequest`. The original frozen five-field currentness request is
captured before source callouts. The existing original-only companions verify
the storage's exact five references, the admission bridge's exact three
references, and the lifecycle composition's actual coordinator object.
The storage root must be the exact original work signal, not an operation or
construction root. The service must be `virtual-realm-admission-v1`.
The account must match both admission identity and partition; the numeric
storage generation must match the admission generation's decimal string.

The frozen handle has only `assertCurrent` and `close`.
`captureRealmAdmissionProtectedStorageSource()` authenticates that original
handle through a private WeakMap before inspecting any operand. Its frozen
four-field companion contains `binding`, native `signal`, `assertCurrent`, and
`assertBinding`. Binding metadata retains the original admission's eight scalar
fields plus `serviceId`. Binding assertion accepts only the original flat
options object, not a copied record, proxy or equal-label substitution.
Historical capture remains available after close; currentness does not.

Currentness rechecks the original upstream proofs and native private/work roots
after every source callout and after diagnostics. Recursive checks reject
without recursive dispatch. A native dependent signal joins the private close,
storage source, admission source and original work roots. Synthetic events and
own signal-property shadows cannot create or hide native retirement.
Terminal state clears retained operational references before own-listener
removal, private abort and diagnostics. Idempotent close publishes its shared
promise before callbacks. It closes no upstream source, manager, boot binding
or context, and never retires a generation or aborts a caller root.

**Legacy-mode limit:** these original companions alone do not prove that the supplied genuine
manager is the adapter's boot-selected manager. Lifecycle issuance also does
not universally authenticate its supplied generation-storage backend.
This cut binding makes neither claim. It does not authenticate an admission
service/result, successful verified load/selection release, policy/trust,
eligibility, prepared CSE commitment or runtime activation. Separate original
boot/adapter lineage and remaining admission-owner proofs must close those
boundaries before the live provider is enabled. The frozen public B4A/B4F
records and sixteen dependencies stay unchanged. First Shard and preview9018
remain outside this work.

One case uses the actual protected view for an absent-only bounded OPFS control

A correctly paired second genuine manager/view is accepted intentionally,
witnessing the explicit remaining boot-selected-manager limitation.

The five flat build pieces are:

   record the original object-root copy, and finalize only after its return.

   first suspension; correlate one actual callback and its genuine lease.

   observe its qualifying exact promise, and return that promise unchanged.

   enrolled original composition, without extending either returned port.

`prepareRealmForgeJsonCloneSource(input)` returns an opaque frozen ticket, not
the input. The private request remains pending while cloning. Each matching
clone entry increments its count before original body callouts; only the first
entry can record a candidate. `finalizeRealmForgeJsonCloneSource(input, ticket,
returned)` closes the attempt and accepts exactly one entry with the matching
original object-root output. Capture is unavailable before finalization. This
also declines same-input reentry in the original dynamic default `Set`
constructor, and a separately re-prepared inner clone cannot match the service's
private ticket. Array roots, passthrough calls, copied/proxy/Promise freeze
substitutes, ignored freeze and altered descriptors get no new clone source.

ordinary legacy clones take no descriptor snapshots and retain no input graph.

callback literal is not the clone's returned value. After ticket-bound original

own data fields and `status: 'accepted'`. Duplicate callbacks, genuine release

leases and any failed release decline completed-load proof while preserving the

The channel operation is non-async and captures the original base method,
rather than dynamically dispatching through the mutable prototype. It calls
that method once and returns its exact outer promise P. The original bare
`return this._withSelection(...)` and catch boundary remain unchanged. The v1
read-port arrow still uses its original dynamic dispatch and adds no observer.
Nested channels save and restore separate private frames; legacy reentry cannot
claim a frame already claimed by its original opt-in invocation.

Attachment requires the original same-realm promise prototype, no own
constructor, and the captured native constructor in the prototype's own data
descriptor. Qualification immediately precedes the private same-realm `await P`;
there is no intervening user callback, `.then` lookup or fallback species route.
The observer catches rejection and resolves only `undefined`, never the result
or rejection reason. After fulfillment it joins exact result identity to the
genuine original lease's catch-free completed-release history. Observation
marks P handled and adds scheduling, as explicitly approved. These claims follow
the ECMAScript [Await](https://tc39.es/ecma262/multipage/control-abstraction-objects.html#await)
and [PromiseResolve](https://tc39.es/ecma262/multipage/control-abstraction-objects.html#sec-promise-resolve)
rules; [ordinary then](https://tc39.es/ecma262/multipage/control-abstraction-objects.html#sec-promise.prototype.then)
has a separate species route.

Trusted pristine kernel-realm initialization is a prerequisite: captured
intrinsics cannot detect replacement before import. Pre-call constructor
accessors or changed constructor data decline attachment; mutation after the
already-attached observation does not revoke historical proof. This is not an
ongoing promise-prototype policy, freshness test, immutable dependency graph,
eligibility or activation capability.

##### Dependency implementation and fresh selection blueprint

This construction remains inside original flat piece4, not a new milestone.
The completed-load channel is accepted only as history. Before an activation
owner can use that result, the kernel must authenticate the implementations that
supplied it and perform a fresh selection check. The human approved the six-piece
catalog build with “continue.” Two additive read-only catalog ports are now
implemented and accepted in this bounded scope. Focused CPU24/24 and native24/24
pass; the full retained native replay passes1000/1000 across37 gates through the
original shared machine lock. They are not installed in the admission composition.
The original ten-piece
construction ledger and visible-city work remain.

The closed scan covers the original service, composition, selection coordinator,
the two shipped registries, policy adapter, M1 adapter, evidence resolver,
signature verifier and context bridge. It follows their literal imports and
accepted inventory metadata, not repository discovery. The reviewed inventory
does not establish an original content, trust-key or native signature-adapter
issuer. An absent issuer in this reviewed cut is not proof that no such code
exists elsewhere; unknown paths remain unopened and First Shard remains excluded.

**Direct service input map.** These are the thirteen actual constructor
dependencies, not thirteen new app dependencies. Sources are
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmPrivateBakeAdmissionService.js` constructor and
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmM2PrivateBakeAdmissionComposition.js` original factory.

| Retained input | Existing implementation or evidence | Remaining implementation requirement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Protected admission storage | Original manager-issued view, boot-selected manager and admission-cut binding are accepted in bounded scope | Preserve exact manager/view/account/generation and live context checks; do not equate a duck-typed scope receipt with original storage authority or claim backend isolation |
| Artifact content | Original loader copies, bounds and hashes exact stored graph bytes | Authenticate the original content issuer and stable bounded read operations; equal hashes do not authenticate arbitrary supplied code |
| Publication head | Original head construction, bridge/writer issuance and consumer retention are accepted as history | Authenticate live head operations and their storage/selection dependencies, preserving exact root/generation/storage-hash comparison |
| Admission policy | `RealmAdmissionPolicyStorageAdapter` implements exact protected reads, policy lineage and currentness | Its mutable fields/prototype and duck-typed dependencies need an original private execution boundary; retain writers privately |
| Evidence-policy registry | Additive private read-only port is bounded-accepted; legacy instance fields and Map remain mutable | Preserve the private verified port when separately reviewing integration; its source authenticates only its shipped catalog implementation |
| Signature trust policy | Loader compares trust ID/revision/digest/generation/storage hash; resolver requests verify-only signing keys | Authenticate trust storage, key resolution and returned native key; neither labels nor a supplied successful callback establish this |
| Runtime-profile registry | Additive private read-only port is bounded-accepted; legacy instance fields and Map remain mutable | Preserve the private verified port when separately reviewing integration; its source authenticates only its shipped catalog implementation |
| Private-bake verifier | Original M1 adapter constructor joins the original entry, evidence resolver and closure verifier | Authenticate executed M1 semantics, evidence and cryptographic implementation; frozen own fields do not freeze prototype dispatch |
| Operator context | Genuine admission context bridge retains original observation/lifecycle and cancellation fences | Use the actual bridge port, original bindings and currentness, not equal scalar snapshots or arbitrary two-method replacements |
| Selection coordinator | Original construction, issued lease and completed release sources are accepted as history | Authenticate native lock operation and original generation hook; mutable coordinator dispatch is not certified by history |
| Contract registry | Default getter is the imported `getVirtualRealmContractDefinition`; custom override is accepted by legacy constructor | Bind the intended catalog implementation and stable lookup; do not authenticate arbitrary overrides by method name |
| Clock | Legacy default delegates to `Date.now`; an arbitrary supplied function remains allowed | Trusted activation-time source and its intended semantics remain separate; no callback is silently promoted to clock authority |
| Random values | Legacy default delegates to `crypto.getRandomValues`; arbitrary supplied function remains allowed | Retain the genuine kernel randomness source when authority IDs are minted; a constructor association is not entropy proof |

The verifier's transitive dependencies remain explicit: the original M1 entry,
evidence-binding store, artifact content, trust/key resolution, signature closure
and signature-adapter factory. Evidence provisioning also retains provisioner,
reviewer, quota and material-source ports. These must be authenticated before
their execution is treated as production admission. They are not new catalog
inputs and are not included in this bounded catalog implementation. The existing
test verifier/policy/trust/context/Map dependencies remain test dependencies.
(Sources: `RealmM1PackageAdmissionAdapter.js`, `RealmAdmissionEvidenceResolver.js`,
`RealmAdmissionEvidenceProvisioningService.js`,
`RealmPrivateBakeProvisioningCoordinator.js` and
`webgpu-os/apps/realmforge/virtual-realm/publication/RealmSignatureClosureVerifier.js`.)

**Implemented and verified catalog APIs.** Factories and
original-only lookups are additive exports inside the two existing registry
owners, rather than a second catalog:
`createRealmAdmissionEvidencePolicyRegistryPort()`,
`captureRealmAdmissionEvidencePolicyRegistryPortSource(port)`,
`createRealmRuntimeCapabilityProfileRegistryPort()` and
`captureRealmRuntimeCapabilityProfileRegistryPortSource(port)`.
Factories accept exactly zero supplied inputs and return a promise. They publish
only after an independent captured-native digest check and the original
verification body complete. They return frozen null-prototype read-only operation
records, with no writer, registration, Map, bytes or owner field. Sources
authenticate only these newly issued implementations. Legacy instances are not
enrolled merely because their constructors ran.

The evidence port preserves the seven existing read/validation operations:
`resolveRegistered`, `assertRegisteredObject`, `resolveReviewerIdentities`,
`resolveRequiredInvariantIds`, `resolveProvisionerRoles`,
`resolveEvidenceKindRule` and `verifyRegistered`. The profile port preserves
`resolveRegistered`, `hasRegisteredId`, `assertRegisteredObject` and
`verifyRegistered`. Synchronous operations stay synchronous; factory verification
and existing asynchronous verification remain asynchronous. Unknown ID/digest
pairs and altered records decline rather than falling back to legacy instances.

Six flat pieces form the approved build, each described in fewer than ten lines:

1. **[NEW in existing owner] Evidence-policy port.** Retain the original shipped
   policy and exact verified bytes privately; reuse original codec/validation
   bodies and expose only the seven operations.
2. **[NEW in existing owner] Runtime-profile port.** Retain the original shipped
   profile and exact verified bytes privately; reuse original validation/digest
   bodies and expose only the four operations.
3. **[NEW] Original implementation lookup.** Use private issuer membership and
   fixed immutable own operations. Reject unknown/copy/proxy candidates before
   reflection and expose no registration or state capability.
4. **[NEW] Mutation and dispatch verification.** Exercise mutable legacy Maps,
   fields, prototype methods, reentry, awaits and returned-record/byte aliases.
   A shallow freeze or pre/post prototype comparison is not sufficient.
5. **[NEW] Preservation verification.** Retain complete original owner bytes,
   old assertion identities and raw-current native execution. Reuse the original
   closed graph, shared lock, HTTP handler and transport bounds.
6. **[MODIFY] Canonical plan and memory.** Record actual source cut, ordered
   receipts and limits only after execution. Do not claim all dependencies are
   authenticated or install either port into the legacy composition yet.

**Private state and stable operation boundary.** The shared internal helper
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmVerifiedCatalogPort.js` reuses the existing preflight
to copy shipped records during trusted pristine module initialization. Captured
own-descriptor operations deeply freeze those copies. Each owner captures its
original method bodies into immutable own operations on a private null-prototype
receiver. Its identity lookup is an immutable single-entry operation, not a
retained mutable Map. Complete policy rules are authorized by the original method
once at initialization and copied privately; later rule lookup uses a captured
own-descriptor operation, not mutable `Array.prototype.find`.

The original `bytesEqual` bodies remain unchanged. Their private comparison
operand is now a frozen null-prototype frame containing only own length and
numeric byte values. Review found that retaining an ordinary TypedArray here
would let a later inherited `byteLength` getter capture and alter its buffer.
The frame closes that escape without changing legacy owners. Native digest
checks retain an independent copy of the original canonical preimage privately,
send only a fresh copy to the captured genuine native digest, and compare the
complete SHA-256 result against the shipped pin. The evidence codec exports a
read-only alias of its existing immutable contract descriptor, avoiding a second
digest definition or schema.

Option-record operations accept zero or one input; omitted or explicit undefined
preserves the original empty-request outcome. Extra operands decline with a fixed
arity error. Supplied options must have only the operation's permitted enumerable
own data fields; identity values are strings or undefined, never coerced objects.
Before an original object-validation body runs, its caller record must match the
complete finite shipped tree by own descriptors, primitive identity, field count,
enumerability and array prototype. The operation then substitutes the private
singleton for the caller reference. Caller-record proxy reflection can run traps
and fail; it is not advertised as callback-free.

Original source capture is different: exactly one candidate is looked up in the
owner's private captured-native WeakMap before any candidate reflection. Unknown,
copied, proxy, revoked, promise and legacy-instance candidates decline with fixed
errors. Its only source operation, `assertImplementation`, accepts exactly zero
inputs and returns true. Each separately issued port gets its own stable source;
helpers exported from the internal helper module cannot enroll into either
owner's inaccessible issuer table. The capture/source operations certify this
specific implementation only, not supplied dependencies, graph loading,
currentness, permission, eligibility or runtime activation.

**Transitive limits.** This is not a claim that every shared codec or schema
validator function is immutable. The original verification bodies deliberately
retain their original dependencies. Later mutable codec, validator or crypto
dispatch can cause an operation to reject. Independent private-tree comparison
and captured-native preimage verification prevent those callbacks from admitting
a different shipped record. Under a throwing late crypto replacement, the
independent native check succeeds but original verification and new issuance
decline; the already issued port's data and implementation source remain stable.
Pristine same-realm kernel initialization is required. No legacy prototype is
frozen or globally replaced, and arbitrary trusted-kernel code execution is not
part of the guarantee. Sources: both registry owners, the new helper and
`RealmAdmissionEvidenceBindingCodec.js`.

Debug coverage uses bounded fixed diagnostics and named case traces around
creation, successful issuance, lookup rejection, validation rejection and elapsed
verification. Diagnostics cannot authorize a port, expose policy/key data or
revive a rejected creation. Preserve original legacy logging and call boundaries.
Rollback is leaving the additive factories uninstalled; never reset or freeze
existing owners. Factory verification adds only the new opted-in operation's
work, not another observer on the accepted v1/v2 load paths.

**Fresh selection remains a separate use boundary.** Original load already
checks request/context/profile, unresolved intents, exact expected heads and the
full stored graph. Inside `runExclusive` it checks lease currentness, refreshes
publication/admission/policy/trust/profile, checks context and checks the lease
again before normalization. After normalization, remaining coordinator checks
concern lease/operator generation, not those full authorities. Drift during
normalization or delayed native request settlement can therefore coexist with
valid historical completed-load proof. This is not a new test failure or a
claim that the original historical source is incorrect.

Never reuse its released lease. A fresh use must acquire a new lease through
the existing native coordinator, retain original context/profile/authority
snapshots and perform the existing exact refresh checks. Result6 and guard8 do
not contain the complete policy/trust generation and storage bindings. Reusing
another original load can revalidate the graph, but its released result is still
only a validation event. Eventual one-shot eligibility must be issued/consumed
at its separately reviewed current-use boundary; a post-release receipt is not
continuing permission. No new mutex or point-in-time promise source replaces
that requirement. (Sources: `RealmPrivateBakeAdmissionService.js`
`_assertAuthoritiesCurrent`, `_withSelection` and `loadVerifiedPrivateBake`;
`RealmAdmissionSelectionCoordinator.js` `runExclusive` and lease release.)

The later fresh-use tests must cover each exact publication/admission/policy/
trust binding, profile rejection, context retirement, waiting behind a genuine
held lease, normalization drift, delayed settlement, truthy/falsy failures and
native cancellation. Historical lookup must add zero reads/lock requests.
Fresh reads, content/cryptographic work, lock contention, callbacks and private
retention require their own explicit reviewed opt-in; this catalog proposal does
not silently add them. Test injections must stay labeled as such.

**Path to the city.** Complete the missing implementation owners and fresh-use
join, then one-shot eligibility, active-bundle/private-handle/prepared-CSE
binding, activation clock/pins/children and recovery. After that, connect the
already built city plan and renderer in the full isolated provider before live
registration. Preserve the source-city workbench and read-only inspector; do not
replace them, rescan their inputs or declare another first-city reset. First-person
travel, local-only operator overview, real code glyphs, permission-safe prebaked
remote silhouettes and SecureMesh station/roads remain the Realm requirements.
This plan introduces no new engine/ECS, camera, NPC or RealmForge authoring work.

##### Admission native support blueprint

The approved build remains inside original flat piece4. It supplies two
stable implementation dependencies from the direct thirteen-input map: admission
wall-time and native randomness. It does not issue activation time, selection,
eligibility or a live provider. The two-catalog implementation remains unchanged;
its historical1000-case receipt retains its original measured scope. The human
approved this six-piece build with “continue build it.” The isolated production
owner is implemented, independently reviewed and bounded-accepted: current CPU24/24,
focused native24/24 and retained native1024/1024 across38 gates pass. This accepts
the new support implementation, not the unresolved catalog descriptor boundary,
integration or a live city.

**Why these dependencies come next.** The current service defaults are closures
that read mutable `Date.now` and `globalThis.crypto.getRandomValues` when called.
The publication bridge uses the same pattern. Existing composition permits
arbitrary supplied functions and records their historical identity, not their
executed implementation. A separate original implementation can capture the
intended native operations without replacing either legacy default or silently
installing functions into those owners. (Sources:
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmPrivateBakeAdmissionService.js` constructor,
`clockMilliseconds`, `safeTimestamp` and `secureOperationId`;
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmPrivatePublicationBridge.js` constructor;
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmM2PrivateBakeAdmissionComposition.js`.)

The closed reuse scan finds a captured native random operation in
`RealmLocalHeadChannel.js`, but that use belongs to its private channel session
creation; it is not an admission entropy export. The existing browser diagnostics
source captures `Performance.prototype.now` and feeds the existing bounded
diagnostic clock. That clock expressly excludes activation/signature time and
persisted authority. The engine's simulation RNG and entropy reports are not
kernel cryptographic entropy sources. None is repurposed or given a wider public
export. Reuse the accepted private issuer mechanism rather than create another
port-membership implementation. (Sources:
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmLocalHeadChannel.js`;
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmBrowserRuntimeDiagnosticsSource.js`;
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmRuntimeDiagnosticsSource.js`;
`engine/core/math/MathRandom.js`; `engine/core/math/MathEntropy.js`;
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmVerifiedCatalogPort.js`.)

Two alternative dependencies remain unresolved. The admission-policy adapter's
read methods delegate to supplied protected-storage operations; freezing the
adapter cannot authenticate those reads. Its writer alone uses the selection
coordinator, so adding writer or coordinator authority is not a prerequisite for
an eventual read-only policy port. Contract definitions are frozen, but their
validation closure still dispatches through the shared schema validator and
semantic/preflight pipeline. Authenticating lookup alone would not authenticate
that validation execution. These findings determine this approved build order,
not an acceptance or fix for either dependency. (Sources:
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmAdmissionPolicyStorageAdapter.js` `readCurrent`, `assertCurrent`,
`_readOptional` and `replaceCurrent`;
`webgpu-os/apps/the-virtual-realm/contracts/RealmContractDefinition.js`
`schemaAssert`, `semanticAssert` and `validateValue`.)

**Implemented owner and interface.** The new isolated owner at
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmAdmissionNativeSupportPort.js` exports
`createRealmAdmissionNativeSupportPort()` and
`captureRealmAdmissionNativeSupportPortSource(port)`.
The factory accepts exactly zero inputs and synchronously returns a frozen
null-prototype record with exactly two immutable own operations, `clock` and
`randomValues`. No caller can choose or replace either backend. Factory/import
performs no time reading, random draw, timer, storage, network or native hashing.
Missing captured backends reject issuance instead of using a fallback or failing
module import.

Before creating the operations or issuing a port, the factory uses captured
native own-descriptor inspection to reject any `get`, `set`, `writable` or
`configurable` control slot on the captured intrinsic Object prototype. Presence
of an own data or accessor property, including an undefined value, rejects with
`VR_ADMISSION_NATIVE_SUPPORT_ISSUANCE_REQUIRED`; no inherited getter is invoked.
This closes descriptor conversion in the unchanged issuer's ordinary literals.
No callback occurs between the guard and synchronous issuance. Existing issued
native-support operations and source lookup do not use that conversion and stay
usable when those slots are later changed. No prototype is frozen or repaired.

Capture the original `Date.now`, `Crypto.prototype.getRandomValues`, the chosen
native Crypto receiver and the native dispatch/branding operations during trusted
pristine kernel-module initialization. Retain these references privately. Later
replacement of global properties or prototype slots must not redirect issued
operations or newly issued ports. This is an explicit trusted-initialization
condition, not a claim to detect arbitrary code installed before initialization
or to infer native authenticity from a function name or its string rendering.

`clock` accepts exactly zero inputs and returns nonnegative safe-integer epoch
milliseconds from the captured native wall-time function. It is synchronous and
performs one native reading per accepted call. Wall time can move backward or be
adjusted by the host. This port provides no monotonicity, UTC canonicalization,
distributed time, secure expiry or persisted activation-clock proof. Existing
admission timestamp conversion and validation stay in their original owners.

`randomValues` accepts exactly one genuine `Uint8Array` with the captured
same-realm base prototype and exactly sixteen bytes in a live, fixed-length,
non-shared buffer with the captured base `ArrayBuffer` prototype. This explicit
opt-in profile matches the existing admission operation-ID caller, not the full
Web Crypto input surface. Captured native slot branding precedes prototype
inspection, so proxies and plain-record lookalikes fail without reflection traps.
Reject non-base prototypes and detached, resizable or shared buffers before native
effects; no coercion or caller getter supplies length, buffer or type. These
checks establish current base-shaped objects, not historical construction
provenance: a genuine subclass instance changed to the base prototype is not
distinguishable by that shape. No callback occurs between final buffer validation
and the captured native fill. Fill the supplied view and return that exact view. Do not
retain its buffer, cache random bytes, sample randomness at creation, add a
statistical entropy test or manufacture uniqueness guarantees. Existing nonzero
and recent-ID rejection stay unchanged in `secureOperationId`.

Original source lookup accepts exactly one issued port and uses the owner's
private captured-native WeakMap before candidate reflection. It reuses
`createRealmVerifiedCatalogIssuer`; the shared helper remains unchanged. Unknown,
copied, proxy, revoked-proxy, promise and unrelated issued ports reject. Its
frozen null-prototype source exposes only exact-zero-input
`assertImplementation()`, returning true for that original support implementation.
It authenticates the whole port, not an arbitrary detached function. Later
composition must retain the genuine port and join its exact own `clock` and
`randomValues` references to the actual service inputs. This build does not make
that join or promote a supplied successful callback to authority.

Six flat pieces define the approved build, each fewer than ten lines:

1. **[NEW] Private native capture.** Add the isolated support owner, SPDX header,
   captured backends and fixed non-secret errors. No import-time native work,
   injected function option, fallback or modification of existing owners.
2. **[NEW] Clock and entropy operations.** Implement the exact two-operation
   profile, native input branding and pre-effect rejection. Preserve same-view
   entropy return and native wall-time semantics without an ID or clock service.
3. **[REUSE] Original implementation lookup.** Use the unchanged shared private
   issuer. Freeze the support record and operations; expose no receiver, writer,
   backend, byte retention, registration or lifecycle capability.
4. **[NEW] Native browser gate.** Cover exact arity, membership and shape, real
   clock/fill operations, post-initialization replacement and buffer branding.
   Restore every test mutation; label injected rejection witnesses as tests.
5. **[REUSE] Preservation and evidence.** Reuse the unchanged native runner,
   source reader, transport bounds and original shared performance lock. Add a
   literal opt-in graph and focused CPU receipt; retain the exact1000-case prefix.
6. **[MODIFY] Acceptance records.** Update these existing canonical pages and
   memory only from completed current receipts. Keep historical catalogs separate
   and leave remaining dependencies, fresh-use joining and provider activation open.

**Verification and rollback.** Pin all complete existing owner/helper bytes before
implementation; new support imports do not enter legacy composition or old
inventories. The opt-in runner may extend only explicitly reviewed literal routes
for the new gate. Keep all old assertion identities and served source rules,
15-second connection/receive bounds,90-second receipt deadline and backlog128.
CPU preservation proves bytes and structure; only actual native browser execution
proves native clock, entropy and buffer behavior. Do not claim a generated module
count, case count, native-profile compatibility or receipt before it is measured.
Independent implementation review precedes execution acceptance.

Use fixed diagnostic families and named per-case traces for issuance, operation
entry/exit, invalid input, source rejection and elapsed test verification. Do not
log timestamps, entropy, operation IDs or caller records, and do not add callback
or console dispatch to a native effect boundary. Diagnostics grant no authority.
Rollback is leaving the isolated owner unimported/uninstalled; never reset,
freeze or overwrite legacy globals or owners in production.

`Array.prototype[Symbol.iterator]` replacement and substitute another candidate.
The implementation now checks exact arity and passes `args[0]` directly, matching
the accepted owner wrappers. The saved native and CPU regression tracks cover
this edge in the completed current receipts below.
No remaining concrete counterexample was found within the trusted-pristine model;
read-only review is not native execution evidence. Source slot/tag checks also
reject other genuine typed-array kinds changed to the Uint8Array prototype.

A final source review found a second concrete seam: captured native
`defineProperty` still converts ordinary descriptor objects using inherited
control fields. A `configurable` getter can substitute a method before its own
`value` is read and before the private issuer enrolls the port. The support-only
pre-issuance guard above closes that seam without changing any preserved owner.
The existing PORT07 and CPU12–14/23 checks are strengthened; all24 named identities
in each track remain unchanged. The updated native cases require previously
issued native-support clock, fill and source operations to remain usable under

factories still issue after the final verification await without this check;
their operation-time descriptor literals also need review. The historical
1000-case receipt remains evidence of its measured coverage, not proof against
this newly uncovered seam. Resolve that catalog boundary before relying on it
for full composition. This stays in original piece4; no shared helper or older
owner is silently changed or newly certified here.

and actual SharedArrayBuffer support, not skip or substitute a fake shared view.

##### Catalog descriptor isolation blueprint

The human's “build it ontinue” approved this six-piece continuation on
2026-10-01. The shared descriptor-conversion change is now implemented inside
original flat piece4, with the explicit current CPU evidence transition described
below. The earlier support build remains historical evidence for its unchanged
helper checkpoint. No new milestone, provider activation or first-city reset is
introduced. Current execution acceptance is recorded separately below this
blueprint; implementation alone is not acceptance.

**Parent cause and scope.** Captured native `Object.defineProperty` still reads
inherited descriptor fields. The parent helper supplied ordinary internal
literals at nine sites: matcher values, request options, isolated request
replacement, receiver fields and methods, byte-frame length and indices, issued
port fields and the implementation source guard. Seven descriptors explicitly
own `enumerable`; two byte-frame descriptors omit it. All nine omit `get`, `set`,
`writable` and `configurable`. Each literal owns `value`. An inherited control
getter can run during the helper's conversion; an inherited `enumerable` getter
also reaches the two byte-frame sites. A factory-only guard does not protect
existing-port calls or changes introduced during permitted proxy reflection or
catalog verification awaits. (Source:
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmVerifiedCatalogPort.js`, all nine `DEFINE` sites;
`RealmAdmissionEvidencePolicyRegistry.js` and
`RealmRuntimeCapabilityProfileRegistry.js`, verified-port factories.)

**Selected implementation.** Add one private descriptor builder to the shared
helper. Wrap each existing fresh internal literal rather than move its anonymous
arrow into a scalar function argument. This preserves inferred arrow names
(`value`) and arrow body source. The builder reads only the literal's own `value`
and `enumerable` descriptors through the already captured native `DESCRIPTOR`.
It creates a fresh record through the already captured `CREATE(null)`, writes own
`value`, `enumerable`, `writable:false` and `configurable:false`, and returns it
directly to captured `DEFINE`. Missing own `enumerable` becomes false. No caller
descriptor, proxy, iterator, spread, coercion, `Object.assign`, prototype mutation,
or ordinary nested `CREATE` descriptor is admitted. The builder remains private;
no export, issuer membership API or authority surface is added.

The actual policy/profile owners and codec stay byte-identical. Native-support's
existing four-field `ISSUANCE_REQUIRED` guard and all operation bodies stay
unchanged. Correct only its obsolete ordinary-descriptor explanation after the
shared helper changes; that comment edit still changes raw bytes and must be
recorded as such. The admission-specific rejection policy is not removed or
widened merely because the shared issuer becomes robust.

##### Admission contract catalog lookup blueprint

**Approved continuation, 2026-10-01.** The human approved this four-piece build
after the descriptor-isolation acceptance. It supplies the contract-registry
lookup implementation inside original piece4. It is not a new activation owner,
milestone or city-plan reset. The separate kernel owner is implemented; current
CPU/native execution, final current-file audit and memory closure are complete
and recorded in the acceptance below. All four approved pieces are complete.
Counts in this original blueprint remain planned specifications, not receipts.

**Actual consumer and scope.** The legacy service retains
`contractRegistry ?? Object.freeze({ get: getVirtualRealmContractDefinition })`.
It passes that object to `buildRealmAdmissionPackageMemberBindings`, whose
consumer calls `contractRegistry.get(definitionName)`, then calls the returned
definition's `validate` and performs encoding/digest work. The required operation
is `get`, not `getDefinition`. Stable lookup does not authenticate the later
validation, encoding or digest implementations. (Sources:
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmPrivateBakeAdmissionService.js`, constructor and both
member-binding calls; `RealmAdmissionPackageIndexCodec.js`,
`buildRealmAdmissionPackageMemberBindings`.)

The existing catalog freezes its ordinary-object name table and exposes the
original getter. That getter indexes the table directly, so inherited names
such as `constructor`, `toString` and `__proto__` can resolve inherited values.
Keep that legacy behavior byte-identical; only the new opted-in port requires
an immutable own data entry. Do not clone the catalog, schemas, examples or
definition/validation closures. (Source:
`webgpu-os/apps/the-virtual-realm/contracts/VirtualRealmContractCatalog.js`.)

**Selected implementation.** A new separate kernel module
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmAdmissionContractCatalogPort.js` imports the existing
frozen name table and original getter, plus the accepted private issuer/error
helper. Trusted pristine initialization captures native own-descriptor,
own-membership, apply and freeze operations, the imported getter and table.
`get(name)` accepts exactly one primitive nonempty string, checks the original
table's own data descriptor, calls the captured original getter synchronously,
and requires the returned identity to equal that descriptor's value. No caller
coercion, callback, supplied catalog, iterator, property getter or await occurs
between membership and original lookup. Unknown/inherited names decline with a
fixed non-secret error. Registered names return the exact original definition.

The synchronous zero-input `createRealmAdmissionContractCatalogPort()` returns
a frozen null-prototype record with only `get`. Its own operation is immutable
and the exposed get function is frozen. Exact-one-input
`captureRealmAdmissionContractCatalogPortSource(port)` uses the reused private
per-owner issuance table before candidate reflection. The source retains only
the existing zero-input `assertImplementation()` guard. Fixed errors use prefix
`VR_ADMISSION_CONTRACT_CATALOG_` and suffixes `ARITY_REQUIRED`, `NAME_REQUIRED`,
`CONTRACT_REQUIRED`, `RESULT_MISMATCH` and `SOURCE_REQUIRED`. No installation,
writer, registration, custom override, new app dependency or runtime authority
is exported.

   transport; retain the entire prior1034 native case sequence unchanged.

   and the retained native profile serially through the original shared lock.
   Measure the new graph; bind raw/served files before/after; reject failures,
   skips, blocked cases, errors and within-run source drift. Compare historical
   continuity explicitly, preserving any outside-slice changes without attribution.
4. **[MODIFY] Canonical acceptance and memory.** Record only completed receipts
   in this foundation, composition, roadmap and memory. Preserve earlier evidence
   as history. Keep validation, remaining dependencies and fresh selection/
   currentness separate; do not activate the provider or advance a city frame.

##### Remaining provider integration and flat construction ledger

Sources: `webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmGpuAllocationCohortCore.js`;
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmOwnerCoupledGpuPresentationPort.js`;
`webgpu-os/apps/the-virtual-realm/runtime/RealmGpuPresentationSyscallAdapter.js`;
`webgpu-os/apps/the-virtual-realm/runtime/RealmGpuPresentationPortContract.js`;
`webgpu-os/apps/the-virtual-realm/rendering/RealmStaticGpuPresenter.js`;
`webgpu-os/kernel/GpuDeviceBroker.js`.

This is deliberately not an incomplete six-field owner handle. Activation
child registration, transfer, and unregistration still require the genuine
activation service, retained per-operator/Realm selection fence, preallocated
single-use tuple receipt slots, and exact terminal resource evidence. No child
methods or process port discriminator are introduced. Full provider registration,
full-provider host isolation, first-person traversal, Operations View, and first frame remain
unaccepted. (Sources:
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmProcessOwnerIdentityAuthority.js`;
`webgpu-os/kernel/realm/RealmLifecycleAllocationAuthority.js`.)

The integration scan also distinguishes reusable mechanisms from missing
authority. `OperatorContext` synchronously invalidates the old generation and
emits `operator:changing` before asynchronous freeze/drain. Its raw events must
be translated to the closed B4A/B4F invalidation records, never forwarded.
`RealmOsLifecycleAdapter` consumes lifecycle allocation/retirement but is not
the genuine owner required by piece 9. StorageManager notifications are not an
atomic head snapshot/subscription protocol. The opted-in owner now supplies
pre-mutation tokens and the separate observation source joins them coherently
to snapshot reads. Standalone boot-owned lifetime is now connected in the
[bounded continuation](#standalone-boot-owned-local-head-lifecycle). Piece 3 still
needs hosted handoff and full-provider composition after its bounded app-port
and isolated-client bindings, not polling or no-op subscriptions.
(Sources: `webgpu-os/kernel/OperatorContext.js`;
`webgpu-os/apps/the-virtual-realm/runtime/RealmOsLifecycleAdapter.js`;
`webgpu-os/storage/StorageManager.js`.)

The participant continuation must also preserve real OS ownership boundaries.
`OperatorContext.registerParticipant()` is boot-time registration, not a runtime
per-app API; `onChange()` invalidation alone does not make switching await Realm
cleanup. Desktop sends suspension directly to an app instance for window and
document conditions; generic visibility events omit per-window minimization.
Generic GPU event-bus messages cannot authenticate a particular owner. A genuine
host callback bridge must connect those sources to the private identity lookup
and queued intents before the full lifecycle port can be accepted. Existing
Entry suspend/resume handling preserves ownership but does not yet pause a real
renderer. (Sources: `webgpu-os/kernel/OperatorContext.js`;
`webgpu-os/shell/Desktop.js`; `webgpu-os/apps/the-virtual-realm/VirtualRealmEntry.js`.)

The existing `PackageHostRealm` supplies an opaque-origin iframe primitive, but
its current bridge cannot carry the Realm provider: Desktop excludes factory
and GPU-panel paths; the bridge drops subscriptions and uncloneable values,
reflectively dispatches syscalls, and has no dedicated bounded provider channel.
Before full activation, isolate the actual app consumer, compose its dedicated
read-only channel and separately reviewed remaining provider routes, and settle
the GPU/renderer placement without exposing raw host authority or falling back
to shared execution. Native OPFS sentinel isolation,
privileged import denial, unknown-message rejection, and cleanup are now tested
by the separate [controlled local-head channel](#controlled-isolated-client-local-head-channel).
That channel does not retrofit this generic package bridge or relocate the renderer.
This remains acceptance work inside pieces 3 and 10, not a new gate or a change
to the frozen sixteen-key dependency record. (Sources:
`webgpu-os/shell/PackageHostRealm.js`; `webgpu-os/shell/Desktop.js`.)

The immediate continuation is piece4's runtime-activation owner after the
[bounded isolated-client channel](#controlled-isolated-client-local-head-channel),
[Entry subscription connection](#entry-owned-operator-and-policy-subscriptions),
[retained-cut B4F adapter](#retained-cut-b4f-policy-adapter-and-lifecycle-notifications)
and [one-shot B4A adapter](#one-shot-b4a-operator-context-adapter), on the accepted
[standalone boot binding](#standalone-boot-owned-local-head-lifecycle), using the
[coherent observation source](#attach-before-read-coherent-local-head-observation)
and the existing
[protected-head observation decision](#protected-head-observation-and-writer-ownership-decision).
Preserve attach-before-read currentness, genuine partition/lifecycle binding
and conservative terminal notifications without exposing writes.
Keep the accepted owner drain during operator transitions and shutdown.
Genuine hosted handoff and full isolated provider/renderer composition still
precede their corresponding live activation; standalone opt-in and the bounded
read-only channel alone are not provider readiness.
Do not reopen accepted pipeline-layout work or treat a point-in-time snapshot
as a live subscription. The following rows are ten flat construction pieces.
They are not renamed B4H subgates or nested contract families.

| Piece | Readiness | Required completion |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1. Protected local-policy head source | Implemented and verified bounded source, reusing the shared transaction helper; B4H remains unaccepted | Keep writes kernel-private and adapt the matching selected Realm's policy head through piece 3. |
| 2. Current-local-Realm and authority-epoch sources | Implemented and verified bounded source: one 4,096-byte per-account selection head with an epoch global across Realm changes | Keep writes private and adapt only the current non-null selection/exact epoch through piece 3; selection grants no ownership or capability. |
| 3. ## M2E: grounded first-person traversal

### Build

- Implement a separate first-person policy and controller.
- Add grounded capsule collision, steps, slopes, head clearance, doors, gates,
  platforms, and collision-safe recovery anchors.
- Add pointer lock, keyboard, mouse, gamepad, focus ownership, and reduced-motion
  policy.
- Add stable object focus, picking, safe text, and read-only inspection.
- Add spatial audio listener and semantic captions.

### Gate

- No traversal path reaches third person, orbit, free flight, director, debug
  camera, detached map, or remote overview.
- Movement cannot penetrate collision, cross a closed gate, or use an unloaded
  navigation cell.
- Pointer-lock escape restores focus and never traps required input.
- Device loss, window blur, bake replacement, and controller disposal preserve
  or restore one verified safe anchor.
- Every required first-person action works without relying on color or audio.

### Rollback

Return to a noninteractive static scene viewer; no alternate traversal camera is
enabled.

## M2F: owner-private Operations View and minimap

### Build

- Validate `LocalOperatorViewPolicyV1` and the current local authority.
- Derive one closed snapshot from the accepted private bake, layout receipt,
  admitted loaded cells, and local lookup.
- Implement bounded isometric/eagle-eye framing, local pan/zoom/focus, and local
  minimap from the same snapshot.
- Add local selection and powerless zone proposals.
- Implement frame-barrier entry and deterministic first-person restoration.

### Gate

- Entry is explicit local input and cannot be triggered by Storylets, links,
  station events, or remote data.
- The snapshot schema has only `localRealmId`; no foreign or viewed Realm field
  exists.
- Public shells, presence, rendezvous, bridges, remote Travelers, remote routes,
  remote object IDs, and remote telemetry are structurally unaddressable.
- Selection and focus are presentation-only.
- Revocation, identity change, policy change, bake change, device loss, and exit
  destroy view resources once and restore a safe first-person anchor.

### Rollback

Disable Operations View independently; grounded first-person remains usable.

## M2G: read-only interaction and proposal path

### Next independent build: plan-bound city inspection

This independent CPU implementation is built and verified while the
[pipeline candidate](#approved-pipeline-layout-implementation) remains
unverified. It is a CPU-only prerequisite inside the existing M2G scope, not
another milestone, an alternate renderer, or acceptance of integrated M2G.
It makes the real compiled city's semantics inspectable before live picking
is connected. It does not move M2E traversal or M2F Operations View to complete.

The reuse scan found the required data already exists:

- The private `buildObjectAndSemanticRecords()` function in `RealmSceneAssembler.js` emits dense plan-local
  `objectIdBindings` and exactly matched `object-role` semantic records.
- `admitRealmStaticRenderPlan()` validates the schema, semantic closure, and
  canonical digest. This is data admission, not active-city or operator authority.
- Bindings explicitly distinguish `stable-object` from `payload-element`.
  Only stable objects carry stable object IDs and presentation slot identity.
  A station box or road element must not acquire a fabricated filesystem ID.
- `runtime/RealmInteractionResolver.js`, absent at the planning checkpoint, now
  implements this independent peer rather than another scene compiler.

Sources: `webgpu-os/apps/the-virtual-realm/rendering/RealmSceneAssembler.js`;
`webgpu-os/apps/the-virtual-realm/rendering/RealmStaticRenderPlanContract.js`.

The following four flat pieces preserve the original bounded build criteria.
The implemented API and current verification state follow the table; acceptance
requires measured evidence, not the existence of the files alone.

| Piece | File and work | Completion evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1. Local input and output shape | Add the already planned `runtime/RealmInteractionResolver.js`. Use existing static-runtime value validation helpers; admit one supplied render plan, bind all queries/results to its exact `renderPlanDigest` and `lifecycleGeneration`, and require a positive integer `objectId`. Keep the validation local; do not extend frozen M0/M1C catalogs or the sixteen app dependencies. | Invalid records and mismatched plan/generation are rejected. ID zero is not a city object; the later pick adapter must handle background separately. A numerically equal object ID from another plan cannot silently resolve in this plan. |
| 2. Deterministic semantic lookup | Join existing object bindings and `object-role` records once. Return an immutable, exact summary containing the requested binding, `sourceKind`, `renderIdentityKey`, and `semanticRole`; include `stableObjectId`, `presentationSlotId`, and `presentationSlotGeneration` only for `stable-object`. A valid but absent ID returns an explicit `not-found` result. | Every object resolves to the matching existing semantic record. `payload-element` never claims stable-object or slot identity. No geometry, canonical plan, ECS state, or source store is modified. Repeated lookups produce identical values. |
| 3. Independent lifetime and diagnostics | Keep construction abortable around asynchronous plan admission, with no retained index on failure. Use idempotent disposal and reject later queries. Reuse bounded project diagnostics for preparation, readiness, cancellation, failure, and disposal; log counts/status, not the plan or source data. | Abort before and during admission leaves no usable resolver. Disposal clears retained plan/index references and is safe twice. Returned summaries remain immutable values, never resource or authority handles. No callback silently substitutes an active-generation claim. |
| 4. Focused verification | Add proposed `tests/virtual-realm/m2-read-only-inspection.test.js`, its matching browser HTML/main entry, and `test_m2_read_only_inspection.py`. Reuse the M2D-A fixture setup and existing contract admission; add independent mapping expectations rather than another bake generator. Inspect and bind only the exact needed import closure before running it. | Positive stable-object and payload-element mappings, absent ID, zero/invalid ID, changed digest/generation, duplicate/mismatched semantics, immutable output, concurrent preparation, cancellation and disposal pass. Record actual counts and source bindings after execution; no predicted pass total. |

The exact request/result shape is now finalized locally in the resolver. It
does not copy entire binding/semantic records indiscriminately.
The first result contains only the fields listed above plus its status and
plan/generation/object binding; resource payloads, filesystem paths, readable
code, glyph content, safe-text bodies, ECS entity handles, GPU handles, and
live objects are not included. The listed presentation-slot identifiers are
inert plan data, not those handles.
Source-backed names or text can be a later explicit store join, not invented
labels. `glyph-style-only` and `projection-role-binding` are not object picks.

Construction consumes the supplied admitted data only: no filesystem scan,
network request, storage access, RealmForge runtime import, GPU allocation,
frame submission, camera creation, or input registration. Keep this peer
unwired to `VirtualRealmEntry`, `RealmStaticGpuPresenter`, action dispatch,
Operations View, and SecureMesh. No synthetic provider or dummy city is allowed.

Live integration still requires the accepted full M2D path, a real identity
attachment pick, current active-bake/generation checks, and the existing M2G
inspection policy. This preparatory resolver is not evidence that a selected
object is currently visible, reachable, accessible, or authorized for action.
Rollback is disposal of this independent CPU peer; the admitted bake, static
plan, draw packets, existing app behavior, and pending pipeline work remain
unchanged. First Shard remains excluded from both the build and its tests.

**Implemented API:** `RealmInteractionResolver` is the module's only export.
Its constructor accepts an optional diagnostic `logger` and no source/service
injection. It exposes three operations:

| Operation | Exact data and behavior |
| --- | --- |
| `prepare({renderPlan, signal})` | Asynchronously re-admits the plan and builds the semantic index. Returns frozen `status: "ready"`, `executionClass: "cpu-inspection-only"`, `renderPlanDigest`, `lifecycleGeneration`, and `objectCount`. |
| `inspect({renderPlanDigest, lifecycleGeneration, objectId})` | Synchronous lookup bound to the exact admitted digest and generation. Returns frozen `status`, digest, generation, and object ID. A resolved result additionally has `sourceKind`, `renderIdentityKey`, and `semanticRole`; only `stable-object` adds `stableObjectId`, `presentationSlotId`, and `presentationSlotGeneration`. A valid absent ID has `status: "not-found"` and no additional fields. |
| `dispose()` | Clears the retained summaries/readiness and logger; returns frozen `{disposed: true}`. Repeated calls return the same receipt. Disposal is permanent, including during pending preparation. |

Preparation and query records may be mutable plain data records, matching the
existing CPU compiler convention. Their fields must be exact, enumerable data
properties with defined values; accessors and extra fields are rejected through
`assertM2ExactRecord()`. Preparation snapshots its outer request before awaiting
plan admission. Queries require positive safe-integer IDs; zero/background and
undefined are not absent-object results. Generation remains the plan's canonical
positive uint64 decimal string, never a converted JavaScript number.

The lifetime is `idle` to `preparing` to `ready`, with failure or cancellation
returning to `idle` for retry. Concurrent preparation rejects with
`VR_M2G_INSPECTION_BUSY`; replacement after readiness rejects with
`VR_M2G_INSPECTION_READY`. Any state can be disposed permanently. The supplied
abort signal controls preparation only; aborting it after successful preparation
does not dispose this independent CPU index. The owner must call `dispose()`.

Diagnostics report preparation, readiness, cancellation/failure and disposal
through bounded `virtual-realm.m2g.inspection.*` events. Fields are execution
class, state, object count and elapsed milliseconds; no plan, query, object
identity, source data, or arbitrary error text is logged. Throwing diagnostic
sinks do not alter normal results; disposal/cancellation triggered by a sink
is rechecked before preparation returns.

Example for a caller that already holds the admitted local plan:

```javascript
import { RealmInteractionResolver } from
  './webgpu-os/apps/the-virtual-realm/runtime/RealmInteractionResolver.js';

async function inspectCompiledCityObject(renderPlan, objectId, signal) {
  const resolver = new RealmInteractionResolver();
  try {
    await resolver.prepare({ renderPlan, signal });
    return resolver.inspect({
      renderPlanDigest: renderPlan.renderPlanDigest,
      lifecycleGeneration: renderPlan.lifecycleGeneration,
      objectId,
    });
  } finally {
    resolver.dispose();
  }
}
```

## M2-GE: optional static Genesis extension

The base M2 certification does not require Genesis. The Living Digital World
track adds a separately switchable `GenesisRealmExtensionManifestV1` bound to
the exact accepted base-bake ID, digest, spatial-layout receipt, variant,
audience, policy, compiler identities, dependency closure, and expiry.

M2-GE may load only:

- the Continuity Core, Foundry District, and Maintenance Works kits;
- reserved sockets for the Possibility Archive, Role Commons, and Culture
  Archive;
- immutable `SoulSeedIdentityRootV1` and `EidosIdentityV1` references;
- static projection bindings, collision, navigation, HLOD, accessibility, and
  local Genesis Operations lookup;
- visibly empty authored infrastructure when no admitted live evidence exists.

The extension activates atomically against one exact base bake. If it is
missing, invalid, incompatible, over budget, or disabled, the accepted base
city remains usable and unchanged. Base-bake replacement either rebinds both
products at one barrier or removes the extension. M2-GE cannot show phenotype,
regulation, homeostasis, reaction, role, culture, lineage, QD, dormancy,
foundry-job, or organismality activity; those begin at M3.

Gate: identical authored inputs produce byte-identical extension layout and
closure; disabling the extension changes no base-bake digest or base runtime
record; and zero unobserved activity is rendered.

## M2H: recovery, performance, accessibility, and integrated acceptance

### Build

- Add the device-recovery barrier and exact resource rebuild registry.
- Add lifecycle, GPU, memory, frame, controller, operations, and semantic-mirror
  evidence collectors.
- Add browser tests for every M2 slice plus the complete first local walk.
- Add bundle/import audits and long-session resource plateaus.
- Freeze reference-tier budgets from measured baselines.

### Integrated gate

1. Launch through WebGPU OS with no ambient dependency.
2. Resolve one owner-partitioned M1C admission index, reconstruct the exact
   private-v2 package, verify its external evidence, and load it without a
   RealmForge runtime import.
3. Spawn exactly once at the Root Spine.
4. Walk the five foundational territories and SecureMesh station shell.
5. Inspect stable objects and sealed Code Matter without source reveal.
6. Enter the own-city Operations View, select one local zone, submit one
   powerless proposal, and return to the preserved first-person anchor.
7. Force device loss during each startup and view state; rebuild exactly once.
8. Shut down, verify zero Realm-owned resources, restart, and reproduce the
   same package digest, inert Storylet catalog digest, static scene digest, and
   stable object IDs.

### Rollback

Disable the Virtual Realm app entry. Accepted bakes, M0-M1C contracts, and other
WebGPU OS applications remain unaffected.
